## 2026-05-09

> Alignment research often has to focus on averting concerning behaviors, but I think the positive vision for this kind of training is one where we can give models and honest and positive vision for what AI models can be and why. I'm excited about the future of this work. https://t.co/4BnLVNjnEY

Quoting https://x.com/AnthropicAI/status/2052808789297115628:
> We found that training Claude on demonstrations of aligned behavior wasn’t enough. Our best interventions involved teaching Claude to deeply understand why misaligned behavior is wrong.
> 
> Read more: https://t.co/0YaRlXhVZb

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/2052928572810256748 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20260518152825/https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/2052928572810256748)

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1566821162113331200 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220905161126/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1566821162113331200)

## 2022-09-03

> One of the bad things about group houses is that they bind relationships closely with a specific place. If the relationships don't have or develop a foundation outside that place, they often fade when people leave it.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1566132782358622208 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220904043428/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1566132782358622208?prefetchTimestamp=1662266068290)

## 2022-08-28

> I hope affirmative action bans cause colleges to finally stop all legacy admissions. Regardless of how you feel about affirmative action, a world without it is surely one in which it's harder for colleges to justify having actively unfair and anti-minority admission practices.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1563690860847304704 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220828052147/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1563690860847304704)

## 2022-08-27 · reply to @xriskology

> @xriskology @WallfacerAG Does it matter what I prioritize? Imagine I prioritize saving elephants, then saving other animals, then people. Others can and should question this prioritization. But I don't think they should say I'm cold and uncaring. They should say that I'm wrong about what's important.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1563666243025924096 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220827231652/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1563666243025924096)

## 2022-08-22 · reply to @AnnaBethLane

> @AnnaBethLane Seems good not to conflate the two. I actually find people pretty on board with the aspects of transhumanism I like too, so I think not all of it is controversial.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561855337614827521 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220822231920/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561855337614827521)

## 2022-08-22 · reply to @jesswhittles

> @jesswhittles Yeah, I feel like the in person community is one I can take or leave. I like the intellectual community, but I can also be more selective with what and who I engage with there. I'm more invested in EA as a philosophy, so I worry more about possible epistemic degradation.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561786124691034115 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220822195311/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561786124691034115)

## 2022-08-22 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> @tallinzen @zehavoc I would actually defend some aspects of transhumanism (though certainly not all) for independent reasons. Like if we had digital humans we should grant them moral status, space exploration seems cool, illness and poverty bad. But those topics seem independent from longtermism.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561723066958303232 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220823022724/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561723066958303232)

## 2022-08-21 · reply to @baykenney

> @baykenney Do you think Charles Manson's approval of murder causes our ongoing efforts to align individuals with the "non-murder" consensus of society to fall apart?

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561476427676913664 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220822092750/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561476427676913664)

## 2022-08-21 · reply to @michael_nielsen

> @michael_nielsen Not with an individual human but with the preferences of humans. Since there are many individual value functions, this places a constraint on the set of agents that can be aligned. I think it's a meaningful constraint on all but the most nihilistic of value aggregation methods.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561472953048604679 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220821221006/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561472953048604679)

## 2022-08-21

> (1) Toasters should behave in ways that align with human preferences. 
> (2) Things with more complex behaviors are harder to align. 
> (3) More impactful things are more important to align. 
> (4) AI will be more complex and impactful than toasters.

Quoting https://x.com/made_in_cosmos/status/1561108495008759816:
> Change my mind: AI alignment makes as much sense as gun alignment, nuclear alignment and internet alignment. The tool doesn’t have any goals of its own, the people using it do. The real problem is people with conflicting goals all having access to extremely powerful tools.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561470282266644481 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220821214952/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1561470282266644481)

## 2022-08-17 · reply to @robertwiblin

> @robertwiblin For me it depends a lot on whether it's a solipsistic experience machine. I get uncomfortable in lucid dreams because I feel like I'm alone and the fun can't really counter that. But if we're all in an experience machine now, I might not want to leave everyone I care about.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1560047912176033794 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220818085151/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1560047912176033794)

## 2022-08-17 · reply to @Aria_Babu

> @Utilit_Aria There are diversity issues in EA, so maybe they just found it hard to articulate where their intuition was coming from and grabbed a weaker argument in defense of it. E.g. black people are super underrepresented in EA relative to their population size within each country.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559955537973743616 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220817205738/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559955537973743616)

## 2022-08-15

> Imagine if society had a disinformation red team. Their job is to disseminate false conspiracy theories to as many people as possible. They document everything they do and eventually reveal the deception. Getting fooled by them is kinda embarassing so everyone tries a bit harder.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559328111002472448 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220816030726/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559328111002472448)

## 2022-08-15 · reply to @sergia_ch

> @sergia_ch People can have a track record of doing costly things that are a good sign of true altruism, e.g. donations and career changes that wouldn't make much sense if they had other motives. These are often good signals of altruistic motives, even though all such signals are imperfect.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559290217122566144 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220815212737/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1559290217122566144)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> @sethlazar You might also think it's not possible to make AGI safe or aligned. But presumably that's not part of the definition of it. So the definition you really have in mind is of an unaligned AGI. If you also think that's the only possible AGI, it seems like that needs further defense.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558697629441134592 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814061223/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558697629441134592)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @sethlazar

> @sethlazar It seems like you're saying that once you've developed unaligned AGI, you're screwed and can't expect to control it with constraints. That's definitely a bad scenario to be in, but most safety/alignment work is trying to prevent that scenario from happening in the first place.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558696298835890178 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814063800/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558696298835890178)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 15. None of the objections in this thread rested on longtermism, nor did any of my replies. I'm happy to assess the merits of longtermism, but nothing we've said here suggests that the case for prioritizing AI safety/alignment has to rest on it. https://t.co/Vv0uatluSH

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558694377861439488 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814055947/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558694377861439488)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 11. I don't see why one would think this is unaccountable, even if you assume no additional symbolic stuff. I agree that there are going to be tangible harms along the way &amp; hope safety/alignment/ethics  techniques can help us identify and prevent them. https://t.co/OKAozyIsdL

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558686387280326656 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814052638/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558686387280326656)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 10. Yes, people who work on empirical AI safety/alignment are probably betting on the possibility we won't see a departure that renders all prior safety work useless. Those who disagree probably pursue theoretical work that seems more paradigm-independent. https://t.co/tlTWNq1QcQ

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558685482535399424 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814060411/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558685482535399424)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 4. Yes, this does seem hard. You'd have to guarantee that all state actors will prevent it, you might need to monitor what private companies and individuals use compute for, etc. But hard policy interventions are not inconsitent with safety/alignment work. https://t.co/d7e4n3zEDv

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558680503519043584 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814063923/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558680503519043584)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 3.  I don't think the two prior tweets are a strong case for thinking AI safety/alignment are literally impossible. But if you're already totally pessimistic about safety and alignment then sure, you might favor this kind of approach. https://t.co/jLLCirvXR2

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558679970326519809 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814050314/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558679970326519809)

## 2022-08-14 · reply to @AmandaAskell

> 2. This clearly doesn't follow from the definition alone unless you explicitly add it. (Constraint: never interact with the system and immediately destroy it.) Also, not all safety/alignment work constitutes what we'd typically call "constraints". https://t.co/z14txjN6go

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558679176713883650 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814054526/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558679176713883650)

## 2022-08-14

> I don't find these arguments against prioritizing work on AI alignment, safety, etc. very compelling. I'll take them one by one.
> 
> 1. I don't think the case for AI risk relies strongly on longtermism. I also don't think AI risk is a distraction or low priority. Let's go! 🧵

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558677553832898560 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814100343/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558677553832898560)

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558594984680366080 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220813232458/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558594984680366080)

## 2022-08-13

> I'd probably be working on AI alignment even if I gave no weight whatsoever to the wellbeing of future people. Don't think the case for AI alignment work depends all that much on longtermism.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558583864116727808 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220814002638/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558583864116727808)

## 2022-08-13 · reply to @casebash

> @casebash Replying "being uncomfortable is part of life" seems too dismissive of an outcome we want to avoid. I don't think you actively desire it, but the reply treats it as an inevitable risk rather than a risk to be minimized. A bit more accurate: "why would you want to risk anyone...?'

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558547808415756288 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220813231943/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558547808415756288)

## 2022-08-13

> Interesting post. It seems like some people want all altruism to be public rather than private (i.e. just taxes). But even if this were ideal, we don't have a functioning global democracy. I'd rather send money to global poverty charities than to governments of wealthy countries.

Quoting https://x.com/RYChappell/status/1558539229986230272:
> Given that billionaires exist, would you rather they spent their money on caviar, private jets and luxury yachts?
> https://t.co/drorVtFkpd

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558540573597843456 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220813194917/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558540573597843456)

## 2022-08-13 · reply to @casebash

> @casebash I agree that calibrating your locations is important. But the downside risk to flirting badly in a bar or nightclub is still pretty high (namely, making the other person feel uncomfortable or anxious). The aptness of the location is far from sufficient for flirting to be welcome.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558538184232906755 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220813193818/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558538184232906755)

## 2022-08-13 · reply to @ShakedDown

> @ShakedDown I think if you make it clear you're trying to respect someone's boundaries but they just aren't feeling it, they're more likely to feel safe to gently reject your advances and give feedback. Aggressive flirting often results in anxiety and the person just leaving for "no reason".

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558536985681113089 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220813193330/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1558536985681113089)

## 2022-08-08

> I hate it when I'm asked to fill out a form with an extremely ambiguous question and it says "IF YOU LIE ON THIS QUESTION YOU ARE BREAKING THE LAW". My current solution is to write down all the reasonable responses to the question and explain each interpretation in the margin.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1556439829537177600 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808134524/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1556439829537177600)

## 2022-08-05

> I'm going to ask the dumb question that's on my mind: have governments ever tried to fight inflation by asking people to spend less for a bit? If the government asked me to spend less to help reduce inflation, I probably would. Like being asked to use less water during a drought.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1555677306479431680 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220805230243/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1555677306479431680)

## 2022-07-29

> I have two default problem-solving modes: hovering so far above the trees that I'm basically on board the ISS, or being so in the weeds that I don't realize there are trees. Developing a smooth zoom function is tricky.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1553116765550366720 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220729203546/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1553116765550366720)

## 2022-07-28

> There should be an "anti-ad" company. If a company screws you over, you can put money into their anti-ad fund. The anti-ad company researches the target market for each company and uses the fund to run ads about why you shouldn't buy their products.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1552715652191768577 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220728180148/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1552715652191768577)

## 2022-07-20

> This touched on an aspect of EA that I quite like. Dashing off to explore is romantic. But successful exploration almost always involves experience, planning, and safety equipment. I care about tackling the mountain, but the best way to do it might only seem sexy in retrospect.

Quoting https://x.com/Aella_Girl/status/1549665982439997440:
> like, there's some wildness i love about the rationalist community, some punk-teen undercurrent that makes me feel kinda nervous and excited. the EAs almost never make me feel awe, they make me feel like im supposed to be in school or investing responsibly

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1549794696393699328 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220720163549/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1549794696393699328)

## 2022-07-20

> Please don't let "maintaining democracy" become a partisan issue. I'd rather live in a democracy governed by my political opponents than an autocracy governed by my political allies.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1549556493082472448 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220720004752/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1549556493082472448)

## 2022-07-17 · reply to @ArtirKel

> @ArtirKel @RichardMCNgo I usually try to discuss a topic and leave personal questions for later. Some of the best first conversations I've had are ones where neither of us knows anything personal about each other at the end. But this also leads to openings for personal info and questions later.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1548722427386945536 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220717173328/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1548722427386945536)

## 2022-07-15 · reply to @crimsonace

> @crimsonace @jasonfurman @StefanFSchubert I mean, if someone thinks it's bad for Amazon then I think should also think it's bad for Kirkland, unless there's some important reason to distinguish the two.
> 
> Presumably regulators could just query Amazon about how they rank products if this is the concern.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1548088520568320001 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220715233537/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1548088520568320001)

## 2022-07-12

> I have a very rough blog draft on when it is and isn't useful to dispute terms and why we do it: e.g. whether AI is intelligent, what AGI is, whether machines know/understand/learn. But it might just turn into a weird love letter to Carnap.

https://x.com/AmandaAskell/status/1546881045798146048 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220712153729/https://twitter.com/AmandaAskell/status/1546881045798146048)

## 2022-02-14

> Phenomenal consciousness = Does it have an inner cinema?
> Self-consciousness = Is it aware of itself?
> Sentience = Can it have positive or negative experiences?
> Moral patienthood = Should we care about what we do to it?
> Moral agency = Should we hold it accountable for what it does?

> @tlbtlbtlb But designing systems with safety principles in mind involves some thinking about what you're going to do and not what you already have. And why limit ourselves to an "off switch"? We don't do that when designing other things. We try to design them so that they function well.

## 2021-01-16

> People did work on aviation safety prior to 1903: they designed planes that could land without killing their pilots. There's always a tradeoff between the concreteness of the system and your ability to do precise safety work. But integrated, early safety work != pure speculation.

Quoting https://x.com/tlbtlbtlb/status/1350554986900574212:
> I think it's counterproductive to do AGI safety research until there's a working system.
> Imagine working on internet safety before 1993. Or aviation safety before 1903. Your mostly-wrong ideas would just slow future people down.

> 
> Could AI become conscious? Yeah, probably. Seems unlikely that animal squish is essential to consciousness.
>

> 
> Would it matter morally if they were? Yup.

> If you talk about toxic workplace conditions, a lot of the leaders will want you out. If a lot of the leaders want you out, they'll find a way to make it happen. If you're a harasser, thats not the case. A lot of the leaders will be A-ok with you being around.
